# Krawl Through Krakow Storefront Context

## Scope

- This workspace contains the WordPress storefront plugin used by Krawl Through Krakow.
- The deployable plugin source is `staging/wp-content/plugins/ktk-storefront`.
- Frontend source is in `staging/wp-content/plugins/ktk-storefront/frontend`.
- Compiled deployable assets are `assets/storefront.js`, `assets/storefront.css`, and the bundled font files.
- OmniLodge is a separate application at `C:\Users\pjcam\Documents\Projects\omni-lodge`. Do not deploy OmniLodge unless the user explicitly requests it.

- All SEO/website improvement work currently authorized by the owner is **staging only**. This supersedes the default production-promotion instruction below for that work. No production or OmniLodge deployment is authorized by the SEO kickoff or repository setup.
- Root `.gitignore` uses an allowlist: KTK-owned code/assets, tools, maintained Markdown and selected immutable public build inputs. Credentials, `Keys`, `wp-config.php`, backups, private operational reports, screenshots, dependencies, third-party WordPress plugins/themes and unknown root directories remain local. - Production WordPress plugin path: `/public_html/wp-content/plugins/ktk-storefront`.

## Deployment Access

- The FTPS credential is stored at `C:\Users\pjcam\.ktk\storefront-ftps-password.xml`.
- The credential file contains a Windows DPAPI-encrypted `SecureString`, tied to the current Windows user and machine. It is not portable plaintext.
- Never display, log, commit, or copy the decrypted password into project files or chat.
- `tools/publish-store2.ps1` decrypts the credential only for the child publisher process and clears the environment variable afterward.
- Production dry run: `.\tools\publish-store2.ps1`.
- Production promotion: `.\tools\publish-store2.ps1 --publish`.
- The underlying publisher is `tools/publish-store2.py`.

## Release Procedure

Unless the user explicitly says local-only or asks not to deploy, finish storefront development by following the staging and production release procedure below.

1. Build and test the storefront locally.
2. Deploy the local 10-file plugin payload to staging, excluding the `frontend` source directory.
3. Back up the remote staging plugin before replacing files.
4. Verify every staging file by SHA-256 and smoke-test the staging page plus compiled CSS and JavaScript.
5. Run the production dry run and require `local_matches_staging: true`.
6. Promote staging to production with the PowerShell wrapper. The publisher backs up production, verifies checksums, confirms plugin activation, and clears the `/store2` WP Rocket cache when present.
7. Smoke-test the production product page and compiled assets with a cache-busting query parameter.
8. After the related PR is merged and the deployment is verified, run `git worktree list`, remove completed temporary worktrees first, then delete the completed local feature branches. Do not remove unrelated or still-active branches/worktrees.
9. Keep local OmniLodge branch hygiene tight: active promotion work for the same feature should live in one `codex/promotion-*` branch, `migration/omni-ha-prep` may remain, and stale completed `codex/*` branches/worktrees should be removed after verification.

## Customer Cart Experience Updates (2026-11-05)

- Customer-owned unpaid drafts keep one complete selection per product ID / experience date / start time. `frontend/src/cart-experiences.js` owns frontend replacement/legacy normalization; the OmniLodge quote service enforces resolved-slot replacement. Latest guests, gender counts and add-ons replace the earlier selection instead of being added together. Different slots remain distinct, including the two NYE groups.
- Product forms prefill existing selections and display Update booking for an existing slot. Stable cart-item journey IDs/numbers survive replacement. Updates offer Undo; another tab's newer selections prevent stale Undo. Updates read newer browser storage so unrelated selections from another tab survive. Explicit cart editing may move a slot and collapses a collision into the latest selection.
- Staff-prepared payment links retain their authored lines and locked behavior. Already-paid orders are preserved; the existing confirmation flow clears the cart and rotates its session for a subsequent purchase. Backend recovery previews/sends reprice legacy duplicates with optimistic status/version checks and audit previous selections.
- Quotes and checkout send cartVersionProtocol 1 with the last acknowledged selection fingerprint. Outdated/superseded drafts require explicit review; the browser keeps its current selections and starts a fresh opaque identity for internal merging, never downloading a different device's private cart. Legacy drafts without a known revision require one review. Recovery hydration stores its acknowledged revision. Same-browser updates read newer local storage and reject stale Undo.
- The PHP proxy preserves OmniLodge's nested error.message and HTTP status, as well as legacy top-level messages, for cart review/payment warnings. `php tools/tests/test_storefront_proxy.php` verifies eight forwarding/error contracts without network calls. The frontend also accepts either error shape for a direct integration.
- Payment confirmation disables ticket/contact/discount edits and removal. The backend checks Stripe before saving/merging a linked cart, rejects stale checkout before cancelling an intent, and rechecks paid state/revision/association under order-then-cart locks. Changed unpaid selections cancel the unsubmitted old intent before creating a separate order/intent; old order snapshots remain for audit. An unchanged intent is reused; creation has an order-and-selection idempotency key. Cancellation/confirmation races preserve the original paid snapshot.
- Verification/release evidence lives in `docs/cart-experience-updates/`; `tools/test-cart-experience-updates.mjs` runs isolated mobile/desktop replacement, Undo and two-tab regressions. It mocks quote/payment/activity endpoints and never sends live orders, emails or payments. Publishing follows the storefront release procedure below. No homepage/content migration files are part of this release.
- Completed staging/production release: OmniLodge PR 238 merged as `d5c41687e3cce5f59f8942057a5329bc87fe91b2`; CI `42088128196`, release `42090567211`, deployment `42092713057` passed. Backend/UI health identify the exact new release. Production has ten checksum-verified plugin files; backup `deploy-backups/ktk-storefront-production-20261105-192803.zip`. Frontend tests 42/42, PHP proxy 8/8, local/staging/production browser checks 18/18 each; backend full suite 340 suites / 3,421 passes / one skipped, final focused tests 85/85. Existing WordPress Facebook reference errors are recorded separately. No real test payments/emails/orders were sent.

JS SHA-256 `d5ed4141f794dcde19a8691c2ee57513df3b034a45f1af4e9973b070d4b93c55`, CSS `e1265119094141579fa8302b1104b81377a31a0fa97e580f12e6387d65e56e21`. Full evidence and known limitations: `docs/cart-experience-updates/README.md`. Homepage/content-page cutover files and Food Tour frontend were untouched by this release.

## Current Storefront Integration Notes

- Product pages receive their catalog and storefront configuration through the product endpoint to avoid a second config request.
- Storefront attribution uses `trk_utm_*`, `trk_gclid`, `trk_gbraid`, and `trk_wbraid` local-storage values. Contact details use `ktk-storefront-contact-v1` and intentionally persist after checkout.
- Add-ons support descriptions and image URLs supplied by OmniLodge. The storefront renders a circular information button and an image/description modal only when that metadata exists.
- Contact forms use a native ISO-country selector with `autocomplete="country"`. Selecting a country name stores its ISO code, while international telephone autofill can also derive and synchronize the country.
- Add-on media is hosted at `https://media.krawlthroughkrakow.com/` in the `ktk-product-media` Cloudflare R2 bucket.
- Storefront deployment does not deploy OmniLodge schema or API changes.
- OmniLodge transport failures and HTTP 502/503/504 responses use the legacy Ecwid store as an availability fallback. Known single-product routes use their matching legacy product URL; multi-product and unmapped carts use `/store/`. Attribution is preserved, and checkout confirmation pages never redirect to the legacy store.
- Checkout confirmations use a session-bound `confirmationToken` returned by OmniLodge. The browser stores it in session storage, confirmation URLs contain no order identifier, and temporary OmniLodge availability failures on the confirmation page are retried instead of redirecting to the legacy store.
- Cart add, edit, update, and remove journey events use a stable non-personal cart item ID and persistent item number. Their snapshots include date/time, participants, named add-ons and variants, and item total; updates include before/after snapshots. These events remain bundled into existing quote/checkout requests.

Supporting source and deployable images/fonts/CSS/JS are in `staging/wp-content/mu-plugins/ktk-homepage-assets/` (`content.php`, `integrations.php`, `site.css`, `site.js`). Publisher recursively backs up/uploads/checksums assets and replaces the main renderer last.

Main booking links use `home_url('/store2/pub-crawl-28/#book')`, so staging handoff stays staging and future production uses the root automatically. A consent-gated `ktk_homepage_booking_click` dataLayer event is NOT a purchase/conversion event.

## Imunify360 Resilience Release (2026-10-21)

- Hosting-level Imunify360 challenged the storefront WordPress REST route and uncached homepage font files. It is not a WordPress plugin or `.htaccess` rule. Account markers date to 2023-06-27 and 2025-04-17, but only the hosting provider can prove the server-wide installer/date. Full evidence and host recommendations are in `docs/imunify-incident-20261021.md`.
- Storefront fallback now recognizes only the exact Imunify 403 and successful non-JSON challenge HTML as upstream availability failures. Ordinary 403/abort behavior is unchanged. Staging and production each have 10 checksum-matched files; JS SHA-256 `a12222cdc2de7066fcbf5f8588747b92f220c7f6e7b306059a9eeb5076b51eca`, CSS unchanged at `e1fbfded953c58363d406380faa5da855e01ba19fa6c47a1118d2755e5168517`. Backups: `deploy-backups/ktk-storefront-staging-20261021-163749.zip` and `deploy-backups/ktk-storefront-production-20261021-163918.zip`. Unit tests passed 32/32 and fallback browser checks 3/3.
- Homepage Passion One 400/700/900 Latin and Latin-ext subsets are embedded in generated `font-resilience.css`; captured origin faces are renamed to `Passion One Origin`. `tools/publish-homepage-shortcode.ps1` rebuilds this stylesheet deterministically. The 981-1099px Store flyout is right-aligned to prevent hidden-menu overflow at 1024px. Local regression passed 1,254/1,254.
- Homepage staging/production each contain the same 232 owned files. Current SHA-256: renderer `ead2162cb3a8ad28313577013e104ead40cd7fa164eaa8e61a1bbd6e1348e521`; resilience CSS `f201564800c2ef0f7d1ef85ac353573e36a29f43971b76a84b4c5fcaa30af662`; parity CSS `2edfdde155c6885b513705d7a1f801e18550c4883d8e88cc49578161c8a98016`; runtime CSS `796f0a616e44de1feec4da2849b89b746979621164ca79c11b49da85542e9654`.
- Verified production rollback archive: `deploy-backups/ktk-homepage-production-20261021-165506-138909.zip`, SHA-256 `95001bc2ed8eb4ad0b58f1cad42924547d1d00edb64a552d6301ae123e0a68d0`. WordPress state hash `766bc3587809b64ede2f0c78cdccd41315e31b907762ccaae77724c4f1e1f125` matched before/after, so no post/settings/global integrations changed.
- Automation callbacks remained challenged. `tools/homepage-imunify-browser-bridge.py` and its PowerShell wrapper use expiring, checksum-verified temporary helpers that a verified normal browser triggers; each helper/status file is removed and its absence checked. Use only when the standard promotion client is blocked, retain before/after state equality, and never weaken the normal production checks.
- The user performed the external cache purge. The post-upload WordPress origin homepage purge succeeded; the WordPress Cloudflare integration was unavailable and did not issue another external purge. Final production browser verification found the expected asset versions, embedded fonts, root canonical, no overflow, zero homepage console errors, and a complete storefront product/booking render with no Imunify screen. Evidence: `docs/imunify-incident-20261021-homepage-production-browser-release.json`.

## Discount Rules Release (2026-10-08)

- The user explicitly authorized OmniLodge and storefront deployment and requested configurable per-code add-on scope. OmniLodge PR 180 merged as `e88d47ff1d218414d0ca8dc9f5454f4f52e84b29`; release `37826332063` and production deployment `37828063452` succeeded. Both backend and UI health identify the new immutable release. This supersedes the earlier storefront release record, not the historical homepage notes.
- Exactly one distinct discount code is allowed. The storefront replaces the selection and normalizes old browser/recovery arrays to the most recent code. The backend rejects multiple distinct codes before quoting/checkout. Historical paid orders, existing coupon amounts and existing payment sessions are unchanged.
- OmniLodge promotion metadata now supports `applicationBasis` (`booking`/`person`) and `discountScope` (`tour`/`tour_and_addons`). Fixed per-person amounts multiply eligible ticket quantities, not add-on counts, with per-line caps. Percentages apply once to eligible charges and are never multiplied by participant count. Old codes default to booking/include-add-ons; new admin codes default to booking/tour-only. No database migration is required.
- Ecwid cannot represent per-person or tour-only rules in the existing adapter. These rules are local-only, guarded on create/update/push/import. Previously synced Ecwid coupons are not automatically deleted or changed. See OmniLodge `docs/storefront-discount-rules.md` before editing shared coupons.
- Staging and production each have 10 verified payload files. JavaScript SHA-256 `c170af852577cc0d35a9d228efd2bfdde1f9112d1c4fc31bb73be8cf73bbdc9e`; CSS remains `e1fbfded953c58363d406380faa5da855e01ba19fa6c47a1118d2755e5168517`. Production backup: `deploy-backups/ktk-storefront-production-20261008-190608.zip`, SHA-256 `47B0AD655288B9BB3BDF1B7B32F37F5BCEC2C75E9E405AFDAD452AC6611D2A55`.
- Publisher staging releases now clear only the exact staging storefront WP Rocket cache paths; production retains its exact store2 path. Three cache-scope tests guard environment isolation and traversal. Cloudflare purges were limited to each environment's store2 and plugin assets prefixes. Production desktop/Android/iPad passed MISS then HIT with current HTML references and byte hashes. No homepage, attribution or unrelated OmniLodge changes were deployed.
- Local checks: backend 259 suites/2,207 passing/one skipped; admin UI 113 suites/759 passing; storefront 29 tests/build; typechecks/lint; isolated admin and cart browser tests. Live staging and production each passed eight responsive cart checks. Quote-only live API checks rejected two codes and confirmed a single 8.33% code gives 19.99 PLN off 240 PLN without creating a cart/order. Evidence and limitations: `docs/storefront-discounts/README.md`. Legacy WordPress Facebook reference errors and generic nested backend error messages remain unchanged.
